Lemma

E.140
allows us to reason about evaluation contexts
in the erasure relation.

Lemma E.140.

If
\Theta;\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{M}[m]\sim m_{0}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({B})
then
there exists
\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\mathcal{M}^{\prime},m^{\prime},A
such that

\Theta=\Theta_{1}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{2}
and

\Theta_{1};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash m\sim m^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({A})
and

m_{0}^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[m^{\prime}]
.
For any
\Theta_{3},n,n^{\prime}
such that

\Theta_{3};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash n\sim n^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({A})
and

\Theta_{2}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{3}
is well-defined, we have

\Theta_{2}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{3};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{M}[n]\sim\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[n^{\prime}]:\mathcal{C}({B})
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of the evaluation context
\mathcal{M}
.

Case
(
\mathcal{M}=[\cdot]
):
Trivial by taking

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=[\cdot]
,
m^{\prime}=m_{0}^{\prime}
,
\Theta_{1}=\Theta
,
\Theta_{2}=\epsilon
, and
A=B
.

Case
(
\mathcal{M}=\textbf{{let}}\;{x}\Leftarrow{\mathcal{N}}\textbf{{ in }}{n}
):
We have the typing judgment

\displaystyle\Theta;\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\textbf{{let}}\;{x}\Leftarrow{\mathcal{N}[m]}\textbf{{ in }}{n_{1}}\sim m_{0}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({B})

By
Lemma

E.113
we have

m_{0}^{\prime}=\textbf{{let}}\;{x}\Leftarrow{m_{1}^{\prime}}\textbf{{ in }}{n_{1}^{\prime}}
and

\displaystyle\Theta;\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\textbf{{let}}\;{x}\Leftarrow{\mathcal{N}[m]}\textbf{{ in }}{n_{1}}\sim\textbf{{let}}\;{x}\Leftarrow{m_{1}^{\prime}}\textbf{{ in }}{n_{1}^{\prime}}:\mathcal{C}({B})

By
Lemma

E.130
there exists

\Theta_{1}^{\prime},\Theta_{2}^{\prime},A^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}^{\prime};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{N}[m]\sim m_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({A^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\Theta_{2}^{\prime};x:A^{\prime};x:_{\textsf{L}}A^{\prime}\vdash n_{1}\sim n_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({B})

\displaystyle\Theta=\Theta_{1}^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{2}^{\prime}\quad x\notin\text{FV}({B})

By the induction hypothesis, there exists
\Theta_{11}^{\prime},\Theta_{12}^{\prime},\mathcal{N}^{\prime},m^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{11}^{\prime};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash m\sim m^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({A^{\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}^{\prime}=\Theta_{11}^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{12}^{\prime}

\displaystyle m_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[m^{\prime}]

and for any
\Theta_{3},n,n^{\prime}
such that

\Theta_{3};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash n\sim n^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({A^{\prime\prime}})
and

\Theta_{12}^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{3}
is well-defined, we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{12}^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{3};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{N}[n]\sim\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[n^{\prime}]:\mathcal{C}({A^{\prime}})

By choosing
\Theta_{1}=\Theta_{11}^{\prime}
,
\Theta_{2}=\Theta_{12}^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{2}^{\prime}
,

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\textbf{{let}}\;{x}\Leftarrow{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}}\textbf{{ in }}{n_{1}^{\prime}}
, and
A=A^{\prime\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{1};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash m\sim m^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({A^{\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle\Theta=\Theta_{1}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{2}

\displaystyle m_{0}^{\prime}=\textbf{{let}}\;{x}\Leftarrow{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[m^{\prime}]}\textbf{{ in }}{n_{1}^{\prime}}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[m^{\prime}]

For any
\Theta_{3},n,n^{\prime}
such that

\Theta_{3};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash n\sim n^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({A^{\prime\prime}})
and

\Theta_{2}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{3}
is well-defined, we know that

\Theta_{12}^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{3}
is well-defined, which means that

\displaystyle\Theta_{12}^{\prime}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{3};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{N}[n]\sim\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[n^{\prime}]:\mathcal{C}({A^{\prime}})

Now applying
Bind
we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{2}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{3};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\textbf{{let}}\;{x}\Leftarrow{\mathcal{N}[n]}\textbf{{ in }}{n_{1}}\sim\textbf{{let}}\;{x}\Leftarrow{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[n^{\prime}]}\textbf{{ in }}{n_{1}^{\prime}}:\mathcal{C}({B})

which concludes this case.
∎

We can now prove the main simulation theorem for processes.

Theorem E.141 (Process Simulation).

Given
\Theta\Vdash P\sim P^{\prime}
and reduction
P\Rrightarrow Q_{0}
, there exists
Q_{0}^{\prime}
such that

P^{\prime}\Rrightarrow Q_{0}^{\prime}
and
\Theta\Vdash Q_{0}\sim Q_{0}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Similar to the proof of session fidelity (
Theorem

E.84
).
We proceed by induction on the derivation of
P\Rrightarrow Q
.

Case
(
Proc-Fork
):

\small{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{fork}}\,({x:A})\,\textbf{{with}}\ {m}]}\rangle}\Rrightarrow{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{m[d/x]}\rangle})}}

By assumption, we have
\Theta\Vdash{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{fork}}\,({x:A})\,\textbf{{with}}\ {m}]}\rangle}\sim P^{\prime}
.
By inversion on the erasure judgment we know there exists
n^{\prime}
such that

P^{\prime}={\langle{n^{\prime}}\rangle}
and

\displaystyle\Theta;\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{fork}}\,({x:A})\,\textbf{{with}}\ {m}]\sim n^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({\textsf{unit}})

By
Lemma

E.140
there exists
\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},\mathcal{N}^{\prime},n^{\prime\prime},B
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{1};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\textbf{{fork}}\,({x:A})\,\textbf{{with}}\ {m}\sim n^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{C}({B})

\displaystyle\Theta=\Theta_{1}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{2}

\displaystyle n^{\prime}=\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[n^{\prime\prime}]

By
Lemma

E.119
we know that
n^{\prime\prime}=\textbf{{fork}}\,({x:A^{\prime}})\,\textbf{{with}}\ {m^{\prime}}
for some
A^{\prime}
and
m^{\prime}
.

By
Lemma

E.135
there exists
A^{\prime\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{1};x:{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle};x:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}\vdash m\sim m^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({\textsf{unit}})

\displaystyle\epsilon\vdash A^{\prime\prime}:\textbf{{proto}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}=\square\quad A={\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}\quad\mathcal{C}({B})\simeq\mathcal{C}({{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}})

By
Channel-HC
we have

d:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash c\sim c:{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}
.

By
Channel-CH
we have

c:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash d\sim d:{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}
.

By
Return
we have

d:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\textbf{{return}}\,{c}\sim\textbf{{return}}\,{c}:\mathcal{C}({{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}})
.
Applying the evaluation contexts
\mathcal{N}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{2},c:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]\sim\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]:\mathcal{C}({\textsf{unit}})

By
Lemma

E.97
we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{1},d:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash m[d/x]\sim m^{\prime}[d/x]:\mathcal{C}({\textsf{unit}})

By
Expr
and
Par
we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{2},d:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle},c:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}\Vdash({\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{m[d/x]}\rangle})\sim({\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{m^{\prime}[d/x]}\rangle})

By
Scope
we have

\displaystyle\Theta\Vdash{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{m[d/x]}\rangle})}}\sim{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{m^{\prime}[d/x]}\rangle})}}

which concludes this case by taking

Q_{0}^{\prime}={\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{m^{\prime}[d/x]}\rangle})}}

and noting that

\displaystyle{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{fork}}\,({x:\square})\,\textbf{{with}}\ {m^{\prime}}]}\rangle}\Rrightarrow{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{m^{\prime}[d/x]}\rangle})}}

Case
(
Proc-End
):

\small{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}[\textbf{{close}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{wait}}\,{d}]}\rangle})}}\Rrightarrow{\langle{\mathcal{M}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}

By assumption, we have

\Theta\Vdash{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}[\textbf{{close}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{wait}}\,{d}]}\rangle})}}\sim P^{\prime}
.
By inversion on the erasure judgment we know there exists
A
and
P^{\prime\prime}
such that

P^{\prime}={\nu{cd}.{P^{\prime\prime}}}
and

\displaystyle\Theta,c:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A}\rangle},d:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A}\rangle}\Vdash({\langle{\mathcal{M}[\textbf{{close}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{wait}}\,{d}]}\rangle})\sim P^{\prime\prime}

By inversion on the erasure judgment we know there exists

\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},m_{1}^{\prime},n_{1}^{\prime}
such that

\Theta=\Theta_{1}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{2}
and

P^{\prime\prime}={\langle{m_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle}\mid{\langle{n_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle}
and

\displaystyle\Theta_{1},c:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{M}[\textbf{{close}}\,{c}]\sim m_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({\textsf{unit}})

\displaystyle\Theta_{2},d:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{wait}}\,{d}]\sim n_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({\textsf{unit}})

By
Lemma

E.140
there exists

\Theta_{11},\Theta_{12},\mathcal{M}^{\prime},m_{2}^{\prime},B
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{11},c:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\textbf{{close}}\,{c}\sim m_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({B})

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}=\Theta_{11}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{12}

\displaystyle m_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[m_{2}^{\prime}]

By
Lemma

E.140
there exists

\Theta_{21},\Theta_{22},\mathcal{N}^{\prime},n_{2}^{\prime},C
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{21},d:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\textbf{{wait}}\,{d}\sim n_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({C})

\displaystyle\Theta_{2}=\Theta_{21}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{22}

\displaystyle n_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[n_{2}^{\prime}]

By
Lemma

E.120
we know that
m_{2}^{\prime}=\textbf{{close}}\,{c}
and
by
Lemma

E.121
we know that
n_{2}^{\prime}=\textbf{{wait}}\,{d}
.

Now following the same reasoning as in the proof of session fidelity
(
Theorem

E.84
) for the
Proc-End
case, we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{12}\Vdash{\langle{\mathcal{M}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}\sim{\langle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}:\mathcal{C}({\textsf{unit}})

\displaystyle\Theta_{22}\Vdash{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}\sim{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}:\mathcal{C}({\textsf{unit}})

\displaystyle\Theta_{11}=\Theta_{21}=\epsilon

By
Par
we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{12}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{22}\Vdash({\langle{\mathcal{M}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle})\sim({\langle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle})

which concludes this case by taking

Q_{0}^{\prime}={\langle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}

and noting that

\displaystyle{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[\textbf{{close}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{wait}}\,{d}]}\rangle})}}\Rrightarrow{\langle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{{()}}]}\rangle}

Case
(
Proc-Com
):

\small{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}[{\textbf{{send}}\,{c}}\;{v}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{recv}}\,{d}]}\rangle})}}\Rrightarrow{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{return}}\,{\langle{{v},{d}}\rangle_{\textsf{L}}}]}\rangle})}}

By assumption, we have

\Theta\Vdash{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}[{\textbf{{send}}\,{c}}\;{v}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{recv}}\,{d}]}\rangle})}}\sim P^{\prime}
.
By inversion on the erasure judgment we know there exists
\kappa
,
A
and
P^{\prime\prime}
such that

P^{\prime}={\nu{cd}.{P^{\prime\prime}}}
and

\displaystyle\Theta,c:_{\textsf{L}}\textsf{ch}^{\kappa}\langle{A}\rangle,d:_{\textsf{L}}\neg{\textsf{ch}^{\kappa}\langle{A}\rangle}\Vdash({\langle{\mathcal{M}[{\textbf{{send}}\,{c}}\;{v}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{recv}}\,{d}]}\rangle})\sim P^{\prime\prime}

By inversion on the erasure judgment we know there exists

\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},m_{1}^{\prime},n_{1}^{\prime}
such that

\Theta=\Theta_{1}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{2}
and

P^{\prime\prime}={\langle{m_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle}\mid{\langle{n_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle}
and

\displaystyle\Theta_{1},c:_{\textsf{L}}\textsf{ch}^{\kappa}\langle{A}\rangle;\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{M}[{\textbf{{send}}\,{c}}\;{v}]\sim m_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({\textsf{unit}})

\displaystyle\Theta_{2},d:_{\textsf{L}}\neg{\textsf{ch}^{\kappa}\langle{A}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{recv}}\,{d}]\sim n_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({A})

By
Lemma

E.140
there exists

\Theta_{11},\Theta_{12},\mathcal{M}^{\prime},m_{2}^{\prime},B
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{11},c:_{\textsf{L}}\textsf{ch}^{\kappa}\langle{A}\rangle;\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash{\textbf{{send}}\,{c}}\;{v}\sim m_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({B})

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}=\Theta_{11}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{12}

\displaystyle m_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[m_{2}^{\prime}]

By
Lemma

E.140
there exists

\Theta_{21},\Theta_{22},\mathcal{N}^{\prime},n_{2}^{\prime},C
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{21},d:_{\textsf{L}}\neg{\textsf{ch}^{\kappa}\langle{A}\rangle};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\textbf{{recv}}\,{d}\sim n_{2}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({C})

\displaystyle\Theta_{2}=\Theta_{21}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{22}

\displaystyle n_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[n_{2}^{\prime}]

By

Lemma

E.103
,

Lemma

E.115
and

Lemma

E.114
we know that

m_{2}^{\prime}={\textbf{{send}}\,{c}}\;{m_{3}^{\prime}}
for some
m_{3}^{\prime}
.

By
Lemma

E.128
we know there exists

\Theta_{111},\Theta_{112},A^{\prime}
and
r
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{111},c:_{\textsf{L}}\textsf{ch}^{\kappa}\langle{A}\rangle;\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash\textbf{{send}}\,{c}\sim\textbf{{send}}\,{c}:\Pi_{t}({x:A^{\prime}}).{\mathcal{C}({B})}

\displaystyle\Theta_{112};\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash v\sim m_{3}^{\prime}:A^{\prime}

By
Lemma

E.122
we know that
m_{3}^{\prime}=v^{\prime}
for some value
v^{\prime}
.

By
Lemma

E.116
we know that
n_{2}^{\prime}=\textbf{{recv}}\,{d}
.

Now following the same reasoning as in the proof of session fidelity
(
Theorem

E.84
) for the
Proc-Com
case, we know there exists
B^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\Theta_{12},c:_{\textsf{L}}\textsf{ch}^{\kappa}\langle{B^{\prime}}\rangle\Vdash{\langle{\mathcal{M}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\sim{\langle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}

\displaystyle\Theta_{22}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{112},d:_{\textsf{L}}\neg{\textsf{ch}^{\kappa}\langle{B^{\prime}}\rangle}\Vdash{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{return}}\,{\langle{{v},{d}}\rangle_{\textsf{L}}}]}\rangle}\sim{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{\langle{{v^{\prime}},{d}}\rangle_{\textsf{L}}}]}\rangle}

\displaystyle\Theta_{111}=\Theta_{21}=\epsilon

By
Par
and
Scope
we have

\displaystyle\Theta_{12}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{22}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{112}\Vdash

\displaystyle\qquad{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}[\textbf{{return}}\,{\langle{{v},{d}}\rangle_{\textsf{L}}}]}\rangle})}}\sim{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{\langle{{v^{\prime}},{d}}\rangle_{\textsf{L}}}]}\rangle})}}

which concludes this case by taking

Q_{0}^{\prime}={\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{\langle{{v^{\prime}},{d}}\rangle_{\textsf{L}}}]}\rangle})}}

and noting

\displaystyle{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[{\textbf{{send}}\,{c}}\;{v^{\prime}}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{recv}}\,{d}]}\rangle})}}\Rrightarrow{\nu{cd}.{({\langle{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{c}]}\rangle}\mid{\langle{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}[\textbf{{return}}\,{\langle{{v^{\prime}},{d}}\rangle_{\textsf{L}}}]}\rangle})}}

Case
(
Proc-
Com
):
Similar to the
Proc-Com
case.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}n}{{\langle{m}\rangle}\Rrightarrow{\langle{n}\rangle}}\textsc{(Proc-Expr)}

By assumption, we have
\Theta\Vdash{\langle{m}\rangle}\sim P^{\prime}
.
By inversion on the erasure judgment we know there exists
m^{\prime}
such that

\Theta;\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash m\sim m^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({A})
and

P^{\prime}={\langle{m^{\prime}}\rangle}
.

By
Theorem

E.139
there exists
n^{\prime}
such that

\Theta;\epsilon;\epsilon\vdash n\sim n^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}({A})
and

m^{\prime}\leadsto\joinrel\mathrel{\mathchoice{\hbox{\hbox to4.09pt{\vbox to4.53pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@discardpath@clipped{M 0 0 L 0 6.27 L 5.66 6.27 L 5.66 0 Z} {{{}{}{{}}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}{\hbox{\hbox to4.09pt{\vbox to4.53pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{{}{}{}{}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@discardpath@clipped{M 0 0 L 0 6.27 L 5.66 6.27 L 5.66 0 Z} {{{}{}{{}}{}

we have
\Theta\Vdash{\langle{n}\rangle}\sim{\langle{n^{\prime}}\rangle}
.

.

Case
(
Proc-Par
):

\frac{P\Rrightarrow Q}{O\mid P\Rrightarrow O\mid Q}\textsc{(Proc-Par)}

By assumption, we have
\Theta\Vdash(O\mid P)\sim P^{\prime}
.
By inversion on the erasure judgment we know there exists
\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2},O^{\prime},P^{\prime\prime}
such that

\Theta=\Theta_{1}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\Theta_{2}
,
P^{\prime}=O^{\prime}\mid P^{\prime\prime}
and

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}\Vdash O\sim O^{\prime}

\displaystyle\Theta_{2}\Vdash P\sim P^{\prime\prime}

By the induction hypothesis, there exists
Q^{\prime}
such that

P^{\prime\prime}\Rrightarrow Q^{\prime}
and
\Theta_{2}\Vdash Q\sim Q^{\prime}
.
By
Par
we have

\Theta\Vdash(O\mid Q)\sim(O^{\prime}\mid Q^{\prime})
.
We conclude this case by taking
Q_{0}^{\prime}=O^{\prime}\mid Q^{\prime}
and noting that

O^{\prime}\mid P^{\prime\prime}\Rrightarrow O^{\prime}\mid Q^{\prime}
by
Proc-Par
.

Case
(
Proc-Scope
):

\frac{P\Rrightarrow Q}{{\nu{cd}.{P}}\Rrightarrow{\nu{cd}.{Q}}}\textsc{(Proc-Scope)}

By assumption, we have
\Theta\Vdash{\nu{cd}.{P}}\sim P^{\prime}
.
By inversion on the erasure judgment we know there exists
P^{\prime\prime}
such that

P^{\prime}={\nu{cd}.{P^{\prime\prime}}}
and

\Theta,c:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A}\rangle},d:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A}\rangle}\Vdash P\sim P^{\prime\prime}
for some
A
.

By the induction hypothesis, there exists
Q^{\prime}
such that

P^{\prime\prime}\Rrightarrow Q^{\prime}
and
\Theta,c:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{ch}}\langle{A}\rangle},d:_{\textsf{L}}{\textbf{{hc}}\langle{A}\rangle}\Vdash Q\sim Q^{\prime}
.
By
Scope
we have

\Theta\Vdash{\nu{cd}.{Q}}\sim{\nu{cd}.{Q^{\prime}}}
.
We conclude this case by taking
Q_{0}^{\prime}={\nu{cd}.{Q^{\prime}}}
and noting that

{\nu{cd}.{P^{\prime\prime}}}\Rrightarrow{\nu{cd}.{Q^{\prime}}}
by
Proc-Scope
.

Case
(
Proc-Congr
):

\frac{P\equiv P_{1}\quad P_{1}\Rrightarrow Q_{1}\quad Q_{1}\equiv Q}{P\Rrightarrow Q}

By assumption, we have
\Theta\Vdash P\sim P^{\prime}
.
By
Lemma

E.138
there exists
P_{1}^{\prime}
such that

\Theta\Vdash P_{1}\sim P_{1}^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime}\equiv P_{1}^{\prime}
.
By the induction hypothesis, there exists
Q_{1}^{\prime}
such that

P_{1}^{\prime}\Rrightarrow Q_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Theta\Vdash Q_{1}\sim Q_{1}^{\prime}
.
By
Lemma

E.138
there exists
Q_{0}^{\prime}
such that

\Theta\Vdash Q\sim Q_{0}^{\prime}
and
Q_{1}^{\prime}\equiv Q_{0}^{\prime}
.
We conclude this case by noting that
P^{\prime}\Rrightarrow Q_{0}^{\prime}

by
Proc-Congr
.

∎
